49  Identity, Workload Trust, and Privileged Access
Identity, Workload Trust, and Privileged Access establishes the infrastructure necessary 
to establish human, workload, device, service, support, and automation identities with 
strong authentication, short-lived authority, and accountable privilege. It fixes 
ownership, state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one 
Strategic Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 50. Identity, Workload Trust, and Privileged Access. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Establish human, workload, device, service, support, and automation identities with strong 
authentication, short-lived authority, and accountable privilege.
Controlled state Principal identity, trust domain, credential, authentication context, role, delegation, privilege session, 
approval, and revocation.
Enforcement boundary Identity proves who or what acts; authorization still evaluates tenant, purpose, object, policy, 
consequence, and current risk.
Continuity rule
When identity assurance, issuer trust, session context, delegation, or privileged activity becomes 
suspect, the platform shall deny or terminate access, revoke credentials, contain reachable 
systems, preserve session evidence, and initiate identity recovery.

50  Policy Enforcement Infrastructure
Policy Enforcement Infrastructure establishes the infrastructure necessary to evaluate 
authorization, classification, purpose, retention, residency, egress, model, tool, and 
consequence policies at material runtime boundaries. It fixes ownership, state, 
boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one Strategic 
Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 51. Policy Enforcement Infrastructure. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, enforcement boundary, 
continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Evaluate authorization, classification, purpose, retention, residency, egress, model, tool, and 
consequence policies at material runtime boundaries.
Controlled state Policy bundle, decision point, enforcement point, input attributes, decision, obligations, version, 
cache, and evidence.
Enforcement boundary Policy enforcement remains external to models and agents and must fail according to the protected 
operation's declared safety posture.
Continuity rule
When policy is unavailable, stale, conflicting, slow, or cannot evaluate required attributes, the 
platform shall deny protected change and data release, allow only explicitly safe cached decisions, 
and expose the policy failure.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
IA-50-001
The infrastructure realization of Policy Enforcement Infrastructure 
SHALL evaluate authorization, classification, purpose, retention, 
residency, egress, model, tool, and consequence policies at 
material runtime boundaries.
Security / 
sovereignty test
Signed control result bound to 
identity, tenant, data class, topology, 
deployment, policy, artifacts, and 
time.
IA-50-002
Every production instance SHALL declare and continuously 
reconcile policy bundle, decision point, enforcement point, input 
attributes, decision, obligations, version, cache, and evidence 
through versioned desired state, observed state, ownership, and 
audit evidence.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, blast radius, state 
boundary, degraded mode, recovery, 
reconciliation, user impact, and 
accountable closure.
IA-50-003
The implementation SHALL enforce this boundary: policy 
enforcement remains external to models and agents and must 
fail according to the protected operation's declared safety 
posture.
Security / 
sovereignty test
Signed control result bound to 
identity, tenant, data class, topology, 
deployment, policy, artifacts, and 
time.
IA-50-004
SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise SHALL preserve the same 
authority, state, isolation, failure, recovery, evidence, replay, and 
human-decision semantics for Policy Enforcement Infrastructure.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, blast radius, state 
boundary, degraded mode, recovery, 
reconciliation, user impact, and 
accountable closure.

agents and must fail according to the protected operation's declared safety posture.

attributes and verify that the platform can deny protected change and data release, allow only 
explicitly safe cached decisions, and expose the policy failure.
• Independent review accepts decision-table coverage, fail-closed tests, bundle rollback, conflict 
resolution, cache expiry, and cross-plane traces for each supported deployment profile.

51  Cryptography, Key Management, and Trust 
Anchors
Cryptography, Key Management, and Trust Anchors establishes the infrastructure 
necessary to protect data, identity, artifacts, communications, audit, and approvals 
through governed cryptographic profiles and customer-compatible key authority. It fixes 
ownership, state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one 
Strategic Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 52. Cryptography, Key Management, and Trust Anchors. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Protect data, identity, artifacts, communications, audit, and approvals through governed 
cryptographic profiles and customer-compatible key authority.
Controlled state Key and certificate identity, algorithm profile, owner, purpose, locality, hardware boundary, rotation, 
revocation, escrow, and evidence.
Enforcement boundary Keys are separated by tenant, domain, environment, purpose, and consequence where required; no 
application owns raw long-lived master key material.
Continuity rule
When key service, trust anchor, algorithm, hardware module, rotation, or revocation state becomes 
unsafe, the platform shall stop affected cryptographic operations, preserve encrypted state, invoke 
approved continuity keys, and re-establish trust before recovery.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

cryptographic profiles and customer-compatible key authority.

time.

Every production instance SHALL declare and continuously 
reconcile key and certificate identity, algorithm profile, owner, 
purpose, locality, hardware boundary, rotation, revocation, 
escrow, and evidence through versioned desired state, observed 
state, ownership, and audit evidence.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, blast radius, state 
boundary, degraded mode, recovery, 
reconciliation, user impact, and 
accountable closure.
IA-51-003
The implementation SHALL enforce this boundary: keys are 
separated by tenant, domain, environment, purpose, and 
consequence where required; no application owns raw long-lived 
master key material.
Security / 
sovereignty test
Signed control result bound to 
identity, tenant, data class, topology, 
deployment, policy, artifacts, and 
time.

52  Secrets, Certificates, and Credential Lifecycle
Secrets, Certificates, and Credential Lifecycle establishes the infrastructure necessary 
to issue, distribute, use, rotate, revoke, and destroy secrets and certificates without 
embedding durable authority in code, images, logs, or user content. It fixes ownership, 
state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one Strategic 
Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 53. Secrets, Certificates, and Credential Lifecycle. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Issue, distribute, use, rotate, revoke, and destroy secrets and certificates without embedding 
durable authority in code, images, logs, or user content.
Controlled state Secret identity, owner, consumer, purpose, scope, issuer, lease, delivery, use evidence, rotation, 
and revocation.
Enforcement boundary Secret delivery must bind to attested identity and workload context; shared static credentials require 
an explicit time-bounded exception.
Continuity rule
When a secret leaks, expires, cannot rotate, resolves to the wrong workload, or remains after 
revocation, the platform shall revoke and contain immediately, rotate dependent authority, identify all 
uses and effects, and prove cleanup.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
IA-52-001
The infrastructure realization of Secrets, Certificates, and 
Credential Lifecycle SHALL issue, distribute, use, rotate, revoke, 
and destroy secrets and certificates without embedding durable 
authority in code, images, logs, or user content.

sovereignty test
Signed control result bound to 
identity, tenant, data class, topology, 
deployment, policy, artifacts, and 
time.

Every production instance SHALL declare and continuously 
reconcile secret identity, owner, consumer, purpose, scope, 
issuer, lease, delivery, use evidence, rotation, and revocation 
through versioned desired state, observed state, ownership, and 
audit evidence.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, blast radius, state 
boundary, degraded mode, recovery, 
reconciliation, user impact, and 
accountable closure.
IA-52-003
The implementation SHALL enforce this boundary: secret 
delivery must bind to attested identity and workload context; 
shared static credentials require an explicit time-bounded 
exception.
Security / 
sovereignty test
Signed control result bound to 
identity, tenant, data class, topology, 
deployment, policy, artifacts, and 
time.

Fault trace, blast radius, state 
boundary, degraded mode, recovery, 
reconciliation, user impact, and 
accountable closure.

53  Data Protection and Confidential Computing
Data Protection and Confidential Computing establishes the infrastructure necessary to 
protect strategic data in transit, at rest, in use, in backup, in telemetry, and during 
external processing according to classification and purpose. It fixes ownership, state, 
boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one Strategic 
Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 54. Data Protection and Confidential Computing. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, enforcement 
boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, and 
assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Protect strategic data in transit, at rest, in use, in backup, in telemetry, and during external 
processing according to classification and purpose.
Controlled state Protection profile, data class, encryption state, execution trust, privacy transform, locality, exposure 
path, and attestation.
Enforcement boundary Confidential processing can reduce infrastructure trust but cannot substitute for authorization, 
minimization, output control, or human accountability.
Continuity rule
When encryption, attestation, privacy transform, memory isolation, or approved execution location 
cannot be established, the platform shall deny protected processing or move to an explicitly 
approved lower-capability mode without exposing the data.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
IA-53-001
The infrastructure realization of Data Protection and Confidential 
Computing SHALL protect strategic data in transit, at rest, in use, 
in backup, in telemetry, and during external processing according 
to classification and purpose.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact manifests, artifacts, 
configuration, topology, workload, 
and environment.

Every production instance SHALL declare and continuously 
reconcile protection profile, data class, encryption state, 
execution trust, privacy transform, locality, exposure path, and 
attestation through versioned desired state, observed state, 
ownership, and audit evidence.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, blast radius, state 
boundary, degraded mode, recovery, 
reconciliation, user impact, and 
accountable closure.
IA-53-003
The implementation SHALL enforce this boundary: confidential 
processing can reduce infrastructure trust but cannot substitute 
for authorization, minimization, output control, or human 
accountability.
System test + review
End-to-end result plus named 
customer or institutional authority, 
exact scope, rationale, conditions, 
and expiry.
IA-53-004
SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise SHALL preserve the same 
authority, state, isolation, failure, recovery, evidence, replay, and 
human-decision semantics for Data Protection and Confidential 
Computing.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, blast radius, state 
boundary, degraded mode, recovery, 
reconciliation, user impact, and 
accountable closure.

54  Tenant, Domain, and Workload Isolation
Tenant, Domain, and Workload Isolation establishes the infrastructure necessary to 
enforce defense-in-depth isolation across identity, compute, memory, accelerator, 
network, storage, key, policy, telemetry, backup, and operations. It fixes ownership, 
state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one Strategic 
Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 55. Tenant, Domain, and Workload Isolation. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, enforcement 
boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, and 
assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Enforce defense-in-depth isolation across identity, compute, memory, accelerator, network, storage, 
key, policy, telemetry, backup, and operations.
Controlled state Isolation profile, enforcement layers, shared resources, residual channels, tests, exception, 
monitoring, and incident state.
Enforcement boundary No single namespace, network, hypervisor, container, application, or contractual control is sufficient 
isolation for protected workloads.
Continuity rule
When any isolation layer fails, a side channel exceeds tolerance, or tenant scope cannot be proven, 
the platform shall fail closed at the nearest enforceable boundary, quarantine shared resources, 
assess cross-domain exposure, and re-establish proof.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
IA-54-001

55  Software Supply Chain and Artifact Integrity
Software Supply Chain and Artifact Integrity establishes the infrastructure necessary to 
admit only traceable, reproducible, signed, scanned, policy-compliant software, models, 
firmware, infrastructure modules, and configuration. It fixes ownership, state, 
boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one Strategic

56  Security Monitoring, Detection, and Response
Security Monitoring, Detection, and Response establishes the infrastructure necessary 
to detect misuse, intrusion, policy violation, data exposure, supply-chain compromise, 
unsafe agent behavior, and control degradation across all planes. It fixes ownership, 
state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one Strategic 
Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 57. Security Monitoring, Detection, and Response. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Detect misuse, intrusion, policy violation, data exposure, supply-chain compromise, unsafe agent 
behavior, and control degradation across all planes.
Controlled state Security signal, detector version, identity and asset context, severity, confidence, case, containment, 
evidence, and closure.
Enforcement boundary Security telemetry is access-controlled evidence and must remain separable from product truth and 
model training unless explicitly governed.
Continuity rule
When critical telemetry is lost, a detector becomes untrusted, or an active threat exceeds automated 
containment authority, the platform shall enter the declared security-degraded state, constrain high-
risk operations, preserve alternate evidence, and escalate to incident command.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

ownership, and audit evidence.

exercise

57  Sovereignty, Residency, and Administrative 
Control
Sovereignty, Residency, and Administrative Control establishes the infrastructure 
necessary to bind data, processing, keys, models, telemetry, support, updates, and 
administrative actions to customer, legal, contractual, and national boundaries. It fixes 
ownership, state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one 
Strategic Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 58. Sovereignty, Residency, and Administrative Control. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Bind data, processing, keys, models, telemetry, support, updates, and administrative actions to 
customer, legal, contractual, and national boundaries.
Controlled state Sovereignty profile, data and processing locality, key authority, administrator jurisdiction, transfer 
path, exception, and evidence.
Enforcement boundary Residency is enforced end to end across primary, replica, cache, backup, log, support, model 
provider, and recovery paths.
Continuity rule
When placement, support, recovery, update, or provider behavior would cross an unapproved 
sovereignty boundary, the platform shall deny the operation, select an approved local path or explicit 
reduced capability, and preserve the blocked reason.
Normative requirements

58  Observability and Telemetry Architecture
Observability and Telemetry Architecture establishes the infrastructure necessary to 
produce correlated metrics, logs, traces, events, profiles, audit references, topology, 
and user-impact state without leaking protected content. It fixes ownership, state, 
boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one Strategic 
Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 59. Observability and Telemetry Architecture. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, enforcement 
boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, and 
assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Produce correlated metrics, logs, traces, events, profiles, audit references, topology, and user-
impact state without leaking protected content.
Controlled state Telemetry schema, source, identity, tenant and domain scope, correlation, sampling, retention, 
quality, and consumer.
Enforcement boundary Operational telemetry supports diagnosis and assurance but cannot replace canonical evidence, 
workflow state, or protected audit records.
Continuity rule
When telemetry gaps, clock errors, sampling bias, correlation loss, or pipeline overload makes 
service state indeterminate, the platform shall surface observability degradation, preserve priority 
signals locally, constrain risky change, and restore complete causal coverage.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

59  Service-Level Management and Error Budgets
Service-Level Management and Error Budgets establishes the infrastructure necessary 
to govern availability, latency, freshness, durability, recovery, quality, safety, and 
human-workflow service objectives by consequence class. It fixes ownership, state, 
boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one Strategic 
Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 60. Service-Level Management and Error Budgets. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Govern availability, latency, freshness, durability, recovery, quality, safety, and human-workflow 
service objectives by consequence class.
Controlled state Service-level indicator, objective, scope, measurement window, exclusions, error budget, burn state, 
owner, and action policy.
Enforcement boundary An SLO is valid only when the measurement covers the user-visible product and explicit partial, 
stale, denied, and degraded states.
Continuity rule
When an error budget burns beyond policy or measurement quality cannot support a fitness claim, 
the platform shall halt or restrict change, prioritize recovery and debt, expose affected product state, 
and require accountable exception.
Normative requirements

60  Capacity, Performance, and Load Engineering
Capacity, Performance, and Load Engineering establishes the infrastructure necessary 
to size and validate compute, accelerator, network, storage, messaging, inference, 
workflow, and human-gate capacity against strategic demand. It fixes ownership, state, 
boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one Strategic

61  High Availability and Fault Tolerance
High Availability and Fault Tolerance establishes the infrastructure necessary to 
maintain declared strategic services through component, node, zone, cell, site, and 
dependency failures without corrupting authority or state. It fixes ownership, state, 
boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one Strategic 
Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 62. High Availability and Fault Tolerance. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, enforcement 
boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, and 
assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Maintain declared strategic services through component, node, zone, cell, site, and dependency 
failures without corrupting authority or state.
Controlled state Availability profile, replica and quorum set, failover policy, health criteria, dependency mode, 
recovery state, and evidence.
Enforcement boundary Redundancy is valid only when replicas do not share an undeclared failure, control, key, network, 
storage, or release domain.
Continuity rule
When a failure exceeds redundancy, quorum is lost, health is ambiguous, or failover would violate 
consistency or sovereignty, the platform shall prefer safe bounded degradation over false 
availability, fence unsafe members, and recover under explicit state reconciliation.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

evidence through versioned desired state, observed state, 
ownership, and audit evidence.

exercise
Fault trace, blast radius, state

62  Backup, Restore, and State Reconciliation
Backup, Restore, and State Reconciliation establishes the infrastructure necessary to 
protect every durable state class with policy-matched backup, immutable recovery 
points, tested restore, integrity validation, and consumer reconciliation. It fixes 
ownership, state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one 
Strategic Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 63. Backup, Restore, and State Reconciliation. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, enforcement 
boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, and 
assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Protect every durable state class with policy-matched backup, immutable recovery points, tested 
restore, integrity validation, and consumer reconciliation.
Controlled state Backup set, source revision, consistency point, encryption, locality, retention, legal hold, restore test, 
and recovery disposition.
Enforcement boundary Backup coverage follows state ownership and includes metadata, schemas, keys, configurations, 
and dependency order needed for usable recovery.
Continuity rule
When a backup is missing, corrupt, unverifiable, outside locality, or cannot restore within objective, 
the platform shall declare the affected recovery posture, protect remaining copies, constrain 
destructive change, and execute alternate recovery.
Normative requirements

Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Restore strategic service across regional, site, control-plane, dependency, and workforce disasters 
within declared recovery objectives.
Controlled state Recovery unit, dependency order, recovery point and time objective, target location, readiness, 
authority, exercise, and declaration.
Enforcement boundary Disaster recovery preserves tenant, sovereignty, key, policy, version, evidence, workflow, and 
decision semantics; it is not only data copying.
Continuity rule
When the primary domain is unavailable or the recovery target cannot prove readiness and 
consistency, the platform shall invoke incident authority, choose the certified recovery path, declare 
data and capability boundaries, and reconcile before reopening.

64  Incident Command and Operational Response
Incident Command and Operational Response establishes the infrastructure necessary 
to coordinate detection, triage, authority, containment, continuity, recovery, 
communication, evidence, decision impact, and learning. It fixes ownership, state, 
boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one Strategic 
Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 65. Incident Command and Operational Response. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Coordinate detection, triage, authority, containment, continuity, recovery, communication, evidence, 
decision impact, and learning.
Controlled state Incident identity, severity, command roles, affected scope, timeline, actions, evidence, customer 
state, decision impact, and closure.
Enforcement boundary Automation may detect and recommend; named humans hold incident command, risk acceptance, 
customer communication, and return-to-service authority.
Continuity rule
When ownership, severity, scope, or decision impact is uncertain during an active incident, the 
platform shall assume the safer consequence class, establish command, constrain effects, preserve 
evidence, and update scope as facts change.

65  Change, Maintenance, and Lifecycle Operations
Change, Maintenance, and Lifecycle Operations establishes the infrastructure 
necessary to execute configuration, patch, scale, migration, certificate, hardware, 
dependency, and maintenance changes through controlled reversible workflows. It fixes 
ownership, state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one 
Strategic Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 66. Change, Maintenance, and Lifecycle Operations. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Execute configuration, patch, scale, migration, certificate, hardware, dependency, and maintenance 
changes through controlled reversible workflows.
Controlled state Change identity, desired diff, affected graph, risk, window, approvers, rollout, observations, rollback, 
and closure.
Enforcement boundary Routine automation does not remove peer review, segregation of duties, customer notice, or stop 
authority for material change.
Continuity rule

approved scope, the platform shall stop the rollout, stabilize the current state, invoke the declared 
recovery path, and reopen approval with observed evidence.

66  Degraded Modes and Continuity of Strategic 
Operations
Degraded Modes and Continuity of Strategic Operations establishes the infrastructure 
necessary to preserve the safest useful strategic capability when dependencies, data, 
models, networks, control services, or sites are impaired. It fixes ownership, state, 
boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one Strategic 
Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 67. Degraded Modes and Continuity of Strategic Operations. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Preserve the safest useful strategic capability when dependencies, data, models, networks, control 
services, or sites are impaired.
Controlled state Degraded-mode identity, trigger, available capability, unavailable capability, trust and freshness 
limits, owner, entry, and exit evidence.
Enforcement boundary Degradation is explicit product state and may never fabricate completeness, freshness, confidence, 
approval, external effect, or successful learning.
Continuity rule
When normal service cannot be maintained or the declared degraded mode itself becomes unsafe, 
the platform shall enter the next safer mode, suspend consequential functions where required, 
maintain human awareness, and recover through verified exit criteria.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

67  Infrastructure-as-Code and Configuration 
Architecture
Infrastructure-as-Code and Configuration Architecture establishes the infrastructure 
necessary to represent infrastructure desired state, policy, topology, deployment 
profiles, and exceptions as reviewed, versioned, testable code and data. It fixes 
ownership, state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one 
Strategic Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 68. Infrastructure-as-Code and Configuration Architecture. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Represent infrastructure desired state, policy, topology, deployment profiles, and exceptions as 
reviewed, versioned, testable code and data.
Controlled state Module and configuration identity, source revision, input schema, plan, policy result, state backend, 
applied revision, drift, and owner.
Enforcement boundary Declarative automation controls infrastructure state but cannot bypass customer authority, runtime 
safety checks, or protected manual ceremonies.
Continuity rule
When desired and actual state drift, state locks fail, plans become stale, or an apply partially 
succeeds, the platform shall stop further mutation, preserve the exact plan and partial effects, 
reconcile under change control, and restore a trusted state backend.

68  Build, Release, Promotion, and Rollback 
Infrastructure
Build, Release, Promotion, and Rollback Infrastructure establishes the infrastructure 
necessary to assemble dependency-closed infrastructure releases and promote 
immutable configurations through evidence-gated environments and cohorts. It fixes 
ownership, state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one 
Strategic Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 69. Build, Release, Promotion, and Rollback Infrastructure. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Assemble dependency-closed infrastructure releases and promote immutable configurations 
through evidence-gated environments and cohorts.
Controlled state Release identity, artifacts, configuration, schemas, policies, models, tests, approvals, cohort, 
promotion, and rollback target.
Enforcement boundary A release is the complete resolved system configuration; mutable tags, floating dependencies, and 
out-of-band production edits are non-conformant.
Continuity rule
When promotion evidence fails, rollout indicators degrade, dependencies drift, or rollback 
compatibility is uncertain, the platform shall halt promotion, contain the cohort, rollback or roll 
forward through an approved target, and preserve all decision evidence.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

reconcile release identity, artifacts, configuration, schemas, 
policies, models, tests, approvals, cohort, promotion, and rollback 
target through versioned desired state, observed state, 
ownership, and audit evidence.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, blast radius, state 
boundary, degraded mode, recovery, 
reconciliation, user impact, and 
accountable closure.

the complete resolved system configuration; mutable tags, 
floating dependencies, and out-of-band production edits are non-
conformant.

conformance test

for exact manifests, artifacts, 
configuration, topology, workload,

69  Fleet, Patch, and Vulnerability Management
Fleet, Patch, and Vulnerability Management establishes the infrastructure necessary to 
maintain complete asset inventory, configuration posture, vulnerability state, patch 
eligibility, exceptions, and evidence across connected and disconnected fleets. It fixes 
ownership, state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one 
Strategic Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 70. Fleet, Patch, and Vulnerability Management. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, enforcement 
boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, and 
assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Maintain complete asset inventory, configuration posture, vulnerability state, patch eligibility, 
exceptions, and evidence across connected and disconnected fleets.
Controlled state Asset identity, hardware and software bill of materials, posture, vulnerability, exploitability, patch, 
cohort, exception, and retirement.
Enforcement boundary Severity alone does not determine action; exploitability, exposure, consequence, compatibility, 
compensating controls, and recovery are evaluated together.
Continuity rule
When a critical exposure emerges, inventory is incomplete, patching fails, or support expires, the 
platform shall contain reachable risk, revoke or isolate affected assets, deploy the certified remedy, 
and track exceptions to closure.

necessary to make infrastructure cost, capacity, energy, carbon, license, provider, and

enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery,

CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION

visible by tenant, capability, product, and deployment profile.
Controlled state Cost and resource ledger, allocation key, rate, reservation, budget, forecast, anomaly, energy, 
carbon, and optimization decision.
Enforcement boundary Economic optimization may not weaken isolation, resilience, sovereignty, quality, evidence, 
accessibility, or human authority.
Continuity rule
When cost or energy exceeds budget, allocation is unreliable, or an optimization would breach a 
control or SLO, the platform shall stop the unsafe optimization, expose trade-offs, apply approved 
demand controls, and route material variance to accountable owners.
Normative requirements

71  Deployment Acceptance and Operational 
Readiness
Deployment Acceptance and Operational Readiness establishes the infrastructure 
necessary to prove that a specific customer deployment can operate, fail, recover, 
secure, explain, support, upgrade, and retire the contracted product scope. It fixes 
ownership, state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while preserving one 
Strategic Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise.
Figure 72. Deployment Acceptance and Operational Readiness. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Prove that a specific customer deployment can operate, fail, recover, secure, explain, support, 
upgrade, and retire the contracted product scope.
Controlled state Acceptance scope, deployment profile, topology, release, tests, open conditions, operators, 
customer controls, evidence, and expiry.
Enforcement boundary Acceptance is scoped to exact artifacts, environment, integrations, data classes, workloads, 
consequence levels, and operating responsibilities.
Continuity rule
When a mandatory test fails, evidence is stale, an open condition exceeds tolerance, or operators 
cannot execute required procedures, the platform shall withhold production authority or restrict the 
accepted scope, record conditions, and retest after verified remediation.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
IA-71-001
The infrastructure realization of Deployment Acceptance and 
Operational Readiness SHALL prove that a specific customer 
deployment can operate, fail, recover, secure, explain, support, 
upgrade, and retire the contracted product scope.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, blast radius, state 
boundary, degraded mode, recovery, 
reconciliation, user impact, and 
accountable closure.
IA-71-002
Every production instance SHALL declare and continuously 
reconcile acceptance scope, deployment profile, topology, 
release, tests, open conditions, operators, customer controls, 
evidence, and expiry through versioned desired state, observed 
state, ownership, and audit evidence.
Fault / recovery 
exercise
Fault trace, blast radius, state 
boundary, degraded mode, recovery, 
reconciliation, user impact, and 
accountable closure.
IA-71-003
The implementation SHALL enforce this boundary: acceptance is 
scoped to exact artifacts, environment, integrations, data classes, 
workloads, consequence levels, and operating responsibilities.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact manifests, artifacts, 
configuration, topology, workload, 
and environment.

72  Infrastructure Governance and Baseline Closure
Infrastructure Governance and Baseline Closure establishes the infrastructure 
necessary to keep infrastructure architecture coherent through ownership, exceptions, 
architecture decisions, lifecycle evidence, metrics, reviews, and controlled amendment. 
It fixes ownership, state, boundaries, degraded behavior, and assurance while 
preserving one Strategic Intelligence Operating System across SaaS, Private Cloud, 
and On-Premise.
Figure 73. Infrastructure Governance and Baseline Closure. The diagram connects infrastructure mandate, controlled state, 
enforcement boundary, continuity rule as one controlled infrastructure contract across admission, operation, degradation, recovery, 
and assurance.
Infrastructure architecture contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Infrastructure mandate Keep infrastructure architecture coherent through ownership, exceptions, architecture decisions, 
lifecycle evidence, metrics, reviews, and controlled amendment.
Controlled state Architecture decision, standard, exception, risk acceptance, review cadence, conformance state, 
amendment, and closure evidence.
Enforcement boundary Technology and provider choices may evolve behind frozen contracts; local implementation cannot 
redefine the Strategic Intelligence Operating System.
Continuity rule
When material divergence, unmanaged debt, repeated exception, obsolete control, or unresolved 
responsibility threatens the baseline, the platform shall constrain affected change, assign 
accountable remediation, elevate semantic conflicts, and amend only through the authority chain.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
IA-72-001
The infrastructure realization of Infrastructure Governance and 
Baseline Closure SHALL keep infrastructure architecture 
coherent through ownership, exceptions, architecture decisions, 
lifecycle evidence, metrics, reviews, and controlled amendment.

conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact manifests, artifacts, 
configuration, topology, workload, 
and environment.

reconcile architecture decision, standard, exception, risk 
acceptance, review cadence, conformance state, amendment, 
and closure evidence through versioned desired state, observed 
state, ownership, and audit evidence.

exercise
Fault trace, blast radius, state 
boundary, degraded mode, recovery, 
reconciliation, user impact, and

and provider choices may evolve behind frozen contracts; local 
implementation cannot redefine the Strategic Intelligence 
Operating System.
Automated 
conformance test
Versioned fixture and signed result 
for exact manifests, artifacts, 
configuration, topology, workload, 
and environment.

review, and amendment.
Component conformance rules
RULE BINDING APPLICATION
Identity precedes product A vendor service, appliance, managed offering, open-source package, or internal implementation becomes 
conformant only through a stable INF component identity and approved manifest.
Ownership remains singular Each component has one accountable infrastructure owner even where operation, hosting, support, key control, or 
incident duties are shared with a customer or provider.
State is explicit Authoritative, derived, temporary, checkpoint, evidence, telemetry, registry, control, and recovery state are 
declared separately and cannot be inferred from the selected technology.
Failure is bounded Every component identifies its containing failure domain, maximum blast radius, degraded behavior, recovery 
order, reconciliation obligations, and terminal escalation.
Evidence follows release Conformance, security, capacity, service-level, resilience, recovery, deployment-parity, and retirement evidence 
resolves to the exact component and released configuration.
CATALOG AUTHORITY
The component catalog freezes infrastructure responsibilities, not products. Technology may be 
substituted when the replacement passes the same state, boundary, isolation, failure, recovery, 
evidence, and deployment-parity contracts.

infrastructure is composed. Every workload manifest selects one class and refines its

ID RUNTIME UNIT SCALE KEY STATE / REPLICATION FAILURE AND RECOVERY

J  SLO and Capacity Metric Catalog
Metrics establish a common operating vocabulary for user journeys, control services, 
state, evidence, security, continuity, capacity, economics, and deployment parity. Each 
SLO profile declares scope, population, measurement, exclusions, threshold, budget, 
action, owner, and evidence.
ID METRIC MEASURED MEANING MANDATORY SCOPE
IM-001 Availability Proportion of eligible user journeys completing in an acceptable 
terminal state
Service + tenant + deployment + 
workload + window

IM-007 Retrieval latency Context and evidence retrieval time by method and scope Service + tenant + deployment +

IM-008 Freshness Age of decision-active state relative to its source and contract Service + tenant + deployment +

IM-009 Collection delay Time from source observation to custody and admission Service + tenant + deployment +

IM-011 Graph projection lag Distance between graph log and queryable projection Service + tenant + deployment +

Service + tenant + deployment +

and evidence explicit. Service-specific failure analyses may add detail but cannot 
remove these control families.

ID FAILURE PRIMARY 
DOMAIN REQUIRED RESPONSE MINIMUM EXERCISE
FM-32 Operator error Administrative 
scope
Stop, recover through controlled workflow,

Privileged operations 
exercise
Degraded-state command rules
COMMAND RULE REQUIRED OPERATING BEHAVIOR
Declare before use Every degraded mode identifies its trigger, authority, available and unavailable capabilities, trust and 
freshness limits, customer impact, maximum duration, and exit evidence.
Protect strategic meaning The system does not represent partial infrastructure, stale data, failed inference, incomplete evidence, lost 
authority, or unreconciled recovery as a complete strategic product.
Constrain effects External and authoritative side effects stop when their identity, policy, idempotency, human gate, 
destination, or effect-reconciliation boundary cannot be established.
Preserve evidence Entry, decisions, automation, human actions, suppressed work, lost signals, fallback use, recovery, and exit 
remain correlated to the incident and affected product state.
Exit through proof Normal service resumes only after controlling dependencies, state, policy, topology, security, capacity, and 
user-visible semantics pass their declared reconciliation and acceptance criteria.
FAILURE DOCTRINE
Availability is never manufactured by hiding degradation. When the safe boundary is uncertain, The 
Eye selects the more restrictive consequence class, preserves human awareness, and restores 
service through tested state.

Constitutional infrastructure architecture matrix
ID INVARIANT BINDING STATEMENT PRIMARY VOLUME 6 
ANCHORS
C-001 Category integrity
The Eye shall be conceived, designed, sold, and operated as a Strategic 
Intelligence Operating System. It shall not be reduced to a chatbot, dashboard, BI 
tool, copilot, or reporting product.
Ch. 1, Ch. 3, Ch. 7, Ch. 72, 
Ch. 4, Ch. 46, Ch. 49
C-002 Closed strategic loop
The product shall implement one continuous operating loop: Observe, 
Understand, Predict, Simulate, Decide, and Learn. No stage may be treated as a 
disconnected feature island.
Ch. 1, Ch. 3, Ch. 4, Ch. 7, Ch. 
46, Ch. 49, Ch. 72
C-003 Product boundary Interfaces such as chat, dashboards, reports, and APIs may exist, but they are 
access modes to the operating system rather than the product definition.
Ch. 1, Ch. 2, Ch. 6, Ch. 40, 
Ch. 48, Ch. 55, Ch. 65, Ch. 67, 
Ch. 68, Ch. 71, Ch. 72, Ch. 3, 
Ch. 4, Ch. 7, Ch. 46, Ch. 49
C-004 Human decision 
sovereignty
A named human authority shall make every final strategic or high-impact decision. 
The system may advise, prepare, and execute approved workflows, but it shall not 
silently assume decision rights.
Ch. 1, Ch. 3, Ch. 4, Ch. 18, 
Ch. 19, Ch. 22, Ch. 41, Ch. 42, 
Ch. 43, Ch. 46, Ch. 49, Ch. 50, 
Ch. 64, Ch. 66, Ch. 72, Ch. 7
C-005 Explain every 
recommendation
Every material forecast, scenario, simulation result, risk, opportunity, and 
recommendation shall expose evidence, assumptions, uncertainty, lineage, 
alternatives, and accountable system versions.
Ch. 18, Ch. 19, Ch. 42, Ch. 43, 
Ch. 46, Ch. 66, Ch. 1, Ch. 3, 
Ch. 4, Ch. 7, Ch. 49, Ch. 72

C-006 Enterprise operating 
standard
The common platform shall satisfy enterprise requirements for governance, 
security, scale, auditability, reliability, and lifecycle management from its first 
production architecture.
Ch. 4, Ch. 8, Ch. 9, Ch. 10, 
Ch. 13, Ch. 22, Ch. 29, Ch. 49, 
Ch. 51, Ch. 54, Ch. 57, Ch. 70, 
Ch. 71, Ch. 1, Ch. 3, Ch. 7, 
Ch. 46, Ch. 72
C-007 Universal core, governed 
specialization
A single domain-neutral core shall serve all customer classes. Domain ontologies, 
models, workflows, agents, and policies may extend the core without forking its 
constitutional semantics.
Ch. 7, Ch. 11, Ch. 13, Ch. 14, 
Ch. 17, Ch. 21, Ch. 23, Ch. 24, 
Ch. 25, Ch. 26, Ch. 27, Ch. 28, 
Ch. 29, Ch. 39, Ch. 42, Ch. 43, 
Ch. 47, Ch. 49, Ch. 50, Ch. 51, 
Ch. 52, Ch. 53, Ch. 54, Ch. 55, 
Ch. 56, Ch. 69, Ch. 31, Ch. 33
C-008 Ten-layer architecture
The ten named architecture layers are the canonical product structure. Cross-
cutting planes may support them but shall not replace, collapse, or rename them 
without constitutional amendment.
Ch. 1, Ch. 2, Ch. 5, Ch. 6, Ch. 
15, Ch. 16, Ch. 39, Ch. 40, Ch. 
45, Ch. 65, Ch. 67, Ch. 68, Ch. 
71, Ch. 13, Ch. 23, Ch. 28, Ch. 
31, Ch. 33, Ch. 49, Ch. 50, Ch. 
51, Ch. 54, Ch. 55
C-009 Knowledge Graph 
centrality
The Knowledge Graph shall be the connected semantic heart of The Eye and the 
shared context substrate for memory, twins, reasoning, prediction, scenarios, 
simulations, and decisions.
Ch. 13, Ch. 23, Ch. 28, Ch. 31, 
Ch. 33, Ch. 49, Ch. 50, Ch. 51, 
Ch. 54, Ch. 55
C-010 Truth-state separation
Observed evidence, extracted claims, inferred relationships, model outputs, 
human judgments, and synthetic scenario data shall remain explicitly 
distinguishable throughout the system.
Ch. 21, Ch. 33, Ch. 47, Ch. 13, 
Ch. 23, Ch. 28, Ch. 31, Ch. 49, 
Ch. 50, Ch. 51, Ch. 54, Ch. 55
C-011 Temporal truth
Material facts and relationships shall preserve event time, observation time, 
validity time, and system-record time so that past world states can be 
reconstructed without hindsight contamination.
Ch. 20, Ch. 31, Ch. 32, Ch. 35, 
Ch. 37, Ch. 38, Ch. 41, Ch. 45, 
Ch. 48, Ch. 13, Ch. 23, Ch. 28, 
Ch. 33, Ch. 49, Ch. 50, Ch. 51, 
Ch. 54, Ch. 55
C-012 End-to-end provenance
Every material intelligence object shall carry source, chain of custody, 
transformation history, ownership, rights, quality, and version lineage back to its 
evidence.
Ch. 31, Ch. 32, Ch. 62, Ch. 13, 
Ch. 23, Ch. 28, Ch. 33, Ch. 49, 
Ch. 50, Ch. 51, Ch. 54, Ch. 55
C-013 Observation breadth The World Observation Layer shall support the full canonical source universe and 
allow governed custom sources without architectural rework.
Ch. 33, Ch. 44, Ch. 13, Ch. 23, 
Ch. 28, Ch. 31, Ch. 49, Ch. 50, 
Ch. 51, Ch. 54, Ch. 55
C-014 Freshness and quality 
contracts
Sources, derived data, twins, models, and intelligence products shall declare 
measurable freshness, completeness, quality, and expiry expectations.
Ch. 34, Ch. 27, Ch. 31, Ch. 35, 
Ch. 36, Ch. 44
C-015 Durable institutional 
memory
The Eye shall preserve institutional context, decisions, assumptions, evidence, 
outcomes, and lessons as durable memory, subject to lawful retention, deletion, 
and customer policy.
Ch. 34, Ch. 27, Ch. 31, Ch. 35, 
Ch. 36, Ch. 44
C-016 Memory governance
Memory shall be permission-aware, tenant-isolated, versioned, retrievable, 
correctable, and protected from unverified model output becoming accepted 
organizational truth.
Ch. 20, Ch. 27, Ch. 31, Ch. 32, 
Ch. 34, Ch. 35, Ch. 37, Ch. 38, 
Ch. 44, Ch. 58, Ch. 62, Ch. 36
C-017 Strategy Graph
Objectives, hypotheses, decisions, initiatives, capabilities, resources, indicators, 
risks, opportunities, and outcomes shall be connected in a governed Strategy 
Graph anchored to the Knowledge Graph.
Ch. 11, Ch. 21, Ch. 27, Ch. 31, 
Ch. 34, Ch. 35, Ch. 36, Ch. 44
C-018 Grounded Digital Twins
Every Digital Twin shall be grounded in evidence, graph entities, temporal state, 
declared assumptions, and calibrated models; it shall never present synthetic state 
as observed fact.
Ch. 28, Ch. 33, Ch. 47, Ch. 53, 
Ch. 57, Ch. 27, Ch. 31, Ch. 34, 
Ch. 35, Ch. 36, Ch. 44
C-019 Twin versionability Digital Twins shall support time travel, branching, comparison, replay, 
reconciliation, and versioned evolution across actual and simulated states.
Ch. 36, Ch. 27, Ch. 31, Ch. 34, 
Ch. 35, Ch. 44
C-020 Canonical prediction 
horizons
The Prediction Engine shall support 30-day, 90-day, 180-day, 1-year, 3-year, and 
5-year horizons with horizon-appropriate methods and uncertainty.
Ch. 14, Ch. 24, Ch. 25, Ch. 26, 
Ch. 49, Ch. 50, Ch. 52, Ch. 19, 
Ch. 41, Ch. 42, Ch. 43
C-021 Probabilistic forecasting
Forecasts shall communicate distributions, confidence, calibration, drivers, 
sensitivity, and failure conditions rather than false precision or unsupported 
certainty.
Ch. 36, Ch. 19, Ch. 41, Ch. 42, 
Ch. 43, Ch. 49, Ch. 50
C-022 Scenario plurality
Scenario Intelligence shall maintain multiple coherent futures, including baseline, 
upside, downside, disruption, and user-defined alternatives; no single forecast 
shall masquerade as destiny.
Ch. 36, Ch. 19, Ch. 41, Ch. 42, 
Ch. 43, Ch. 49, Ch. 50
C-023 Reproducible simulation
Every simulation shall declare initial state, assumptions, interventions, model 
versions, constraints, random seeds where applicable, outputs, and sensitivity so 
results can be reproduced and challenged.
Ch. 36, Ch. 19, Ch. 41, Ch. 42, 
Ch. 43, Ch. 49, Ch. 50
C-024 Decision completeness
Decision Intelligence shall compare viable options against explicit objectives, 
constraints, risks, opportunities, trade-offs, second-order effects, reversibility, and 
information value.
Ch. 18, Ch. 19, Ch. 41, Ch. 42, 
Ch. 43, Ch. 44, Ch. 45, Ch. 46, 
Ch. 50, Ch. 49
C-025 Decision Replay
The Eye shall preserve the state of knowledge available at decision time and 
support replay against later evidence and outcomes without rewriting the historical 
record.
Ch. 31, Ch. 33, Ch. 34, Ch. 35, 
Ch. 36, Ch. 38, Ch. 44, Ch. 53, 
Ch. 57

C-026 Objective alignment
Recommendations shall be evaluated against declared objectives, stakeholder 
obligations, risk appetite, policy, and strategic constraints rather than an opaque

C-027 Executive Operating 
System

evidence, objectives, commitments, and outcomes. It shall not degrade into a 
passive dashboard collection.

Ch. 57
C-028 Bounded agent authority

budgets, escalation rules, prohibited actions, evaluation suite, and accountable 
owner.

traces across multi-agent work.

C-030 Model pluralism
The platform shall support multiple models and reasoning approaches behind 
governed interfaces so customers retain control over cost, sovereignty, 
performance, and vendor dependence.

C-031 No silent self-modification Continuous learning shall never permit unreviewed production models, agents, 
prompts, policies, ontologies, or decision logic to modify themselves silently.
Ch. 46, Ch. 56, Ch. 58, Ch. 59, 
Ch. 61, Ch. 64, Ch. 66
C-032 Proactive intelligence
The system shall surface material changes, weak signals, risks, and opportunities 
before users ask, while respecting relevance, authority, attention, and notification 
policy.
Ch. 46, Ch. 56, Ch. 58, Ch. 59, 
Ch. 61, Ch. 64, Ch. 66
C-033 Early-warning discipline
Warnings shall be deduplicated, prioritized, evidence-backed, confidence-aware, 
time-bounded, and linked to affected objectives, assets, decisions, and response 
playbooks.
Ch. 31, Ch. 34, Ch. 35, Ch. 36,

C-034 Decomposable Strategic 
Health Score
Any Strategic Health Score shall expose component measures, weights, evidence, 
confidence, trend, and sensitivity. The score shall never replace the underlying 
analysis.
Ch. 5, Ch. 8, Ch. 12, Ch. 13, 
Ch. 15, Ch. 17, Ch. 23, Ch. 39, 
Ch. 54, Ch. 61, Ch. 63, Ch. 46,

C-035 Trust as a system 
property
Trust, provenance, identity, authorization, policy, safety, and audit shall operate 
across every layer and intelligence object, not as a downstream compliance add-
on.
Ch. 16, Ch. 18, Ch. 19, Ch. 20, 
Ch. 22, Ch. 24, Ch. 25, Ch. 27, 
Ch. 30, Ch. 32, Ch. 35, Ch. 37,

Ch. 70, Ch. 56, Ch. 64
C-036 Non-repudiable 
accountability
Material access, transformations, agent actions, approvals, decisions, overrides, 
releases, and exports shall produce tamper-evident audit records attributable to 
identities and versions.
Ch. 3, Ch. 4, Ch. 7, Ch. 8, Ch. 
9, Ch. 10, Ch. 11, Ch. 12, Ch. 
14, Ch. 21, Ch. 23, Ch. 26, Ch. 
29, Ch. 51, Ch. 53, Ch. 57, Ch. 
63, Ch. 60, Ch. 61, Ch. 62
C-037 Customer data control
Customers retain governance over their data, models, twins, policies, retention, 
residency, encryption, exports, and deletion within the chosen deployment and 
contractual boundary.
Ch. 20, Ch. 37, Ch. 48, Ch. 8, 
Ch. 9, Ch. 10, Ch. 11, Ch. 12, 
Ch. 14, Ch. 60, Ch. 61, Ch. 62, 
Ch. 63
C-038 Open interoperability
The Eye shall integrate through governed APIs, events, connectors, common data 
formats, and export paths; core customer knowledge shall not be trapped behind 
proprietary-only access.
Ch. 5, Ch. 8, Ch. 12, Ch. 15, 
Ch. 16, Ch. 17, Ch. 22, Ch. 29, 
Ch. 30, Ch. 39, Ch. 59, Ch. 60, 
Ch. 61, Ch. 63, Ch. 70, Ch. 9, 
Ch. 10, Ch. 11, Ch. 14, Ch. 62
C-039 Zero-trust security
Identity, device, workload, agent, model, source, and data access shall be 
continuously authenticated, authorized, scoped, logged, and re-evaluated under 
least privilege.
Ch. 56, Ch. 58, Ch. 59, Ch. 64, 
Ch. 69, Ch. 8, Ch. 9, Ch. 10, 
Ch. 11, Ch. 12, Ch. 14, Ch. 60, 
Ch. 61, Ch. 62, Ch. 63
C-040 Privacy and minimization
Collection and use shall be purpose-bound, proportionate, policy-enforced, and 
minimized, with support for consent, legal basis, access, correction, retention, and 
deletion obligations.
Ch. 59, Ch. 60, Ch. 8, Ch. 9, 
Ch. 10, Ch. 11, Ch. 12, Ch. 14, 
Ch. 61, Ch. 62, Ch. 63
C-041 Sovereignty by design
Data residency, key ownership, network isolation, local inference, model choice, 
administrative control, and disconnected operation shall be supported where 
customer policy requires them.
Ch. 5, Ch. 12, Ch. 15, Ch. 16, 
Ch. 20, Ch. 22, Ch. 30, Ch. 32, 
Ch. 34, Ch. 37, Ch. 41, Ch. 59, 
Ch. 60, Ch. 61, Ch. 62, Ch. 63, 
Ch. 66, Ch. 8, Ch. 9, Ch. 10, 
Ch. 11, Ch. 14
C-042 Deployment semantic 
parity
SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise deployments shall preserve the same core 
semantics, governance model, APIs, artifacts, and intelligence lifecycle, with 
documented capability differences only where unavoidable.
Ch. 55, Ch. 56, Ch. 58, Ch. 62, 
Ch. 64, Ch. 65, Ch. 67, Ch. 68,

C-043 Resilient operation
The platform shall fail safely, degrade intelligibly, recover predictably, preserve 
evidence and audit continuity, and expose when data, models, or dependencies 
are unavailable or stale.

C-044 Governed learning loop

through evaluation, approval, release, monitoring, rollback, and tenant-isolation 
controls.

O  Infrastructure Architecture Decision Baseline
These decisions freeze the governing infrastructure architecture beneath Volumes 0, 3, 
4, and 5. Implementations may change providers and technologies behind them but 
cannot treat a local exception as authority to change their semantics.
ADR DECISION BINDING CONSEQUENCE IMPLEMENTATION OBLIGATION
IADR-001 One infrastructure contract across 
deployment profiles
Physical implementations vary; strategic 
semantics and conformance do not.
Implement through owned components, 
manifests, external controls, conformance 
evidence, immutable releases, monitoring, 
recovery, and audit.
IADR-002 Cell-oriented isolation and failure 
containment
Tenant and domain workloads execute in 
bounded intelligence cells.
Implement through owned components, 
manifests, external controls, conformance 
evidence, immutable releases, monitoring, 
recovery, and audit.
IADR-003 Management plane separated from 
customer data planes
Fleet control cannot become an unrestricted 
customer-data path.
Implement through owned components, 
manifests, external controls, conformance 
evidence, immutable releases, monitoring, 
recovery, and audit.
IADR-004 Workload identity precedes network 
trust
Network location alone never authorizes 
service access.
Implement through owned components, 
manifests, external controls, conformance 
evidence, immutable releases, monitoring, 
recovery, and audit.
IADR-005 Policy enforcement external to models 
and agents
AI behavior cannot expand infrastructure 
authority.
Implement through owned components, 
manifests, external controls, conformance 
evidence, immutable releases, monitoring, 
recovery, and audit.
IADR-006 State-class architecture precedes 
storage technology
Authority, consistency, locality, recovery, and 
lifecycle select the store.
Implement through owned components, 
manifests, external controls, conformance 
evidence, immutable releases, monitoring, 
recovery, and audit.
IADR-007 Canonical state separated from 
indexes and caches
Derived acceleration remains rebuildable and 
visibly stale.
Implement through owned components, 
manifests, external controls, conformance 
evidence, immutable releases, monitoring, 
recovery, and audit.
IADR-008 Durable execution for material multi-
step work
Workflow and agent progress survives 
process and infrastructure failure.
Implement through owned components, 
manifests, external controls, conformance 
evidence, immutable releases, monitoring, 
recovery, and audit.
IADR-009 Explicit messaging delivery semantics Retries, ordering, replay, and institutional 
effects remain testable.
Implement through owned components, 
manifests, external controls, conformance 
evidence, immutable releases, monitoring, 
recovery, and audit.
IADR-010 Accelerators are governed shared 
infrastructure
Artifact, tenancy, topology, capacity, and 
attestation are controlled.
Implement through owned components, 
manifests, external controls, conformance 
evidence, immutable releases, monitoring, 
recovery, and audit.
IADR-011 Runtime sandboxes enforce agent and 
connector reachability
Instructions cannot grant file, network, secret, 
or side-effect access.
Implement through owned components,

IADR-012 Registered egress instead of generic 
internet access

receipts are explicit.

evidence, immutable releases, monitoring,

options

control and sovereignty.

evidence, immutable releases, monitoring,

models, firmware, and configuration

supply chain.

evidence, immutable releases, monitoring,

P  Infrastructure Glossary
These terms are canonical for deployment, compute, network, state, runtime, trust, 
reliability, recovery, delivery, operations, economics, and governance. Domain designs 
may add narrower terms but may not redefine the concepts below.
TERM INFRASTRUCTURE ARCHITECTURE DEFINITION
Admission A controlled decision that a workload, request, release, artifact, or state transition may enter a defined 
runtime scope.
Air-gapped deployment A deployment profile with no direct connectivity to external networks and controlled physical or 
mediated transfer.
Artifact attestation Evidence that a specific executable, model, firmware, or configuration artifact has approved identity 
and integrity.
Availability domain A location or infrastructure grouping designed to fail independently within a declared dependency 
model.
Blast radius The maximum actual or potential scope affected by a fault, compromise, change, or resource 
condition.
Cell A bounded execution and state topology that contains tenant, domain, capacity, release, and failure 
scope.
Checkpoint An immutable compatible recovery point for a workflow, agent, stream, job, simulation, or other 
durable execution.
Cold start The latency and resource state required to activate a previously inactive runtime or artifact.
Confidential computing Attested hardware and runtime protection intended to reduce exposure of data while it is processed.
Consistency model The declared visibility, ordering, concurrency, and failure behavior of reads and writes.
Control plane Infrastructure that governs identity, policy, configuration, registry, release, fleet, or operational desired 
state.
Control service A service that issues, evaluates, or reconciles governing state rather than owning strategic product 
truth.
Customer control Authority retained by a customer over identity, keys, networks, administrators, data, updates, 
operations, or acceptance.
Data plane Infrastructure that processes customer inputs and strategic intelligence objects under externally 
enforced control.
Degraded mode An explicit restricted operating state with declared triggers, capabilities, limitations, authority, and exit 
evidence.
Deployment profile A certified physical and responsibility realization of the common product contract.
Desired state The approved declarative infrastructure configuration a controller is required to realize.
Disconnected operation Operation with denied or intermittent external connectivity and planned local continuity and 
reconciliation.
Disruption budget The maximum permitted concurrent or temporal workload unavailability during controlled operations.
Egress A mediated outbound transfer from a workload or trust zone to an external destination.
Error budget The permitted measured unreliability inside an SLO window and the policy that governs its 
consumption.
Evidence vault Protected storage for release, audit, acceptance, recovery, incident, and operational assurance 
evidence.
Failure domain A bounded set of components expected to share a causal infrastructure failure.
Failover Controlled transfer of service or state ownership to a certified alternate after failure.
Failback Controlled return from a recovery target to the intended primary topology after reconciliation.
Fencing A mechanism preventing an unsafe or stale member from performing authoritative operations.
Fleet The complete set of managed physical and virtual infrastructure assets in a defined scope.
Headroom Capacity reserved above measured demand for burst, failure, recovery, change, and uncertainty.
Immutable release A dependency-closed configuration whose referenced artifacts and policies do not change after 
publication.
=== PAGE 192 ===
THE EYE VOLUME 6  /  INFRASTRUCTURE ARCHITECTURE
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED INFRA BASELINE V1.0 PAGE 192
TERM INFRASTRUCTURE ARCHITECTURE DEFINITION
Infrastructure component A stable accountable infrastructure responsibility that may be realized by one or more technologies.
Infrastructure manifest The versioned contract for a component's state, dependencies, controls, failure, recovery, and 
evidence.
Infrastructure-as-Code Versioned declarative source used to plan, review, apply, and reconcile infrastructure state.
Ingress A controlled inbound access path from a user, customer system, source, or external service.
Intelligence cell A cell hosting bounded Strategic Intelligence Operating System workloads and their scoped 
dependencies.
Locality The physical, cloud, regional, site, jurisdictional, or hardware location constraint on data or 
processing.
Management plane The shared infrastructure that manages fleets, releases, configuration, policy distribution, and 
operations.
Noisy neighbor A shared-resource workload whose consumption impairs another tenant, domain, or workload 
contract.
Observed state Measured infrastructure state reconciled against approved desired state.
Operational reserve Capacity and authority deliberately retained for failure, recovery, incidents, and change.
Parity Preservation of product, authority, state, failure, evidence, replay, and lifecycle semantics across 
deployment profiles.
Placement The controlled assignment of a workload or state replica to infrastructure satisfying all declared 
constraints.
Private Cloud The deployment profile operated inside a customer-controlled cloud boundary using certified 
dependencies.
Quorum The minimum set of members required to authorize a consistent distributed operation.
Recovery point objective The maximum accepted loss of committed state measured from the recovery point.
Recovery time objective The maximum accepted time to restore a declared recovery unit to its usable state.
Reconciliation The controlled comparison and repair of desired, observed, authoritative, derived, or recovered state.
Residency The approved physical or jurisdictional location of data, processing, keys, backups, telemetry, or 
support access.
Restore Reconstruction of usable state from a recovery set followed by integrity and consumer reconciliation.
Runtime unit A deployable or executable infrastructure pattern with explicit state, scaling, failure, and recovery 
behavior.
SaaS The managed multi-region deployment profile operated by The Eye under a shared-responsibility 
model.
Semantic parity Identity of strategic meaning and authority despite permitted physical or operational implementation 
differences.
Service-level indicator A governed measure of a user-visible or control-critical service property.
Service-level objective A target for an indicator over a declared scope, population, and time window.
Side effect An operation that changes external, institutional, customer, or authoritative state beyond the 
executing runtime.
Site A customer or provider physical location containing a bounded deployment topology.
Sovereignty Control over where and under whose authority data, processing, keys, administration, providers, and 
support operate.
State class A controlled category defining ownership, authority, consistency, durability, locality, protection, and 
recovery.
Supply-chain provenance Traceable evidence from source and builder through dependencies to a released artifact.
Tenant A customer isolation and administrative scope preserved across identity, policy, compute, network, 
state, keys, telemetry, and operations.
Topology The declared graph of locations, cells, components, dependencies, routes, failure domains, and 
recovery relationships.
Trust anchor A protected root used to establish cryptographic, identity, artifact, or execution trust.
Unit economics Cost and resource consumption attributed to a controlled customer, capability, workflow, product, or 
workload unit.
Workload identity A short-lived attested identity bound to a specific executable runtime and trust domain.
=== PAGE 193 ===
THE EYE VOLUME 6  /  INFRASTRUCTURE ARCHITECTURE
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED INFRA BASELINE V1.0 PAGE 193
TERM INFRASTRUCTURE ARCHITECTURE DEFINITION
Workload manifest The deployable contract for identity, artifacts, resources, placement, connectivity, state, controls, 
failure, and lifecycle.
Zone A network, availability, trust, or locality boundary with explicit membership and permitted flows.
Baseline closure
Volume 6 closes when every production capability can identify its deployment profile, customer and 
platform responsibility, topology, cell, runtime units, state classes, network zones, workload manifests, 
identities, policies, keys, artifacts, capacity, service levels, failure domains, degraded modes, backup, 
recovery, incident path, change path, release, cost, acceptance, evidence, exception, and retirement 
state.
END STATE
The Eye is deployable here as one enterprise Strategic Intelligence Operating System: physically 
adaptable, semantically invariant, sovereign by design, isolated by default, accelerated where 
justified, evidence-bound in operation, explicit in degradation, recoverable through tested state, and 
always subordinate to accountable human authority.
